# Review log: Session suspend, idle suspension, and resume driver

## Standing context

### Settled

DECISION: A `resuming` incarnation is identified by the `coordination_generation` its `resume_pending → resuming` write increments to, and every non-terminal exit is conditioned on `resuming` at that value — BECAUSE the previous state-plus-unchanged-generation condition was ABA-prone (the watchdog exit bumps nothing, so a re-entry restored the pair) — ALTERNATIVES: `UpdatedAt` as the attempt token (rejected: `AdvanceLastSeq` writes the row asynchronously after every event Publish, so a token taken at entry is invalidated before the commit; EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessionevents/events.go:278-283; cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:270-275), `recovery_generation` (rejected: client-visible, and §7.2 snapshot-close step 4(b) says an interrupted attempt mints none), a new column (excluded).
DECISION: The SPEC-1b claims "at most one attempt per session at a time" and "an attempt ends no later than the `resuming` watchdog's deadline" are deleted — BECAUSE drivers on two replicas both claim before the entry write, and CODE-4 item 3.4 resets the attempt timer while `sweepResuming` anchors on an `UpdatedAt` every write advances (watchdog.go:775). Neither claim is needed once the exits are conditioned.
DECISION: The `sweepResuming` exit is guarded on state plus the listed row's `coordination_generation`, computes the retry branch from the locked row, and returns `errResumingSuperseded` on a miss (CODE-4 item 15) — ALTERNATIVES: an `UpdatedAt` equality guard (ABA-safe because `UpdatedAt` strictly advances, but rejected so that every `resuming` exit uses one condition and no Postgres microsecond round-trip test is needed). EVIDENCE: watchdog.go:777-803.
DECISION: The OPEN item "lease taken without `RecordHandoff`" ([spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]) is closed — BECAUSE the entry write increments the generation under a compare-and-swap on the value read before the lease was confirmed, so the incarnation's generation is unique however the lease was acquired, and two drivers that read the same value cannot both enter. EVIDENCE: coordination_lease.go:34-45; coordination.go:524-541.
DECISION: The OPEN item "two releasers on a mid-resume terminal" ([spec.2.review-mechanism.1]) is closed — BECAUSE the replacement pod is in neither `PodAssignment` nor the registry before the commit (CODE-4 item 4), and §7.2 snapshot-close step 3 already sends the reclaim "on the connection that attempt still holds"; the attempt is the only gateway releaser. The controller-side orphan-claim GC is covered by the orphan-claim DECISION below.
DECISION: The WPC orphan-claim GC stays the reclaimer of a replacement pod whose attempt stopped. Its drain of a live attempt older than `claimOrphanTimeout` is an accepted outcome, and SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** states it. No new mechanism is added — BECAUSE the pod is outside `pod_assignment` until the restore finishes on both resume branches today (resume_rebind.go:102, :129; pod_launch.go:208; bind_registration.go:39, :64), so CODE-4 adds no exposure. The GC is the only reclaimer of a crashed attempt's pod. CODE-4 item 3.4's deadline ends a live attempt `MaxResumingSeconds` after its entry write, which follows the claim's `bound` stamp (podclaim/bindingstate.go:72-73; claimer.go:219), so with equal 300 s defaults (watchdog.go:155; gc.go:34) the overlap is limited to the entry latency and the release. A drained live attempt only reports a failure after the claim and spends a retry — ALTERNATIVES: writing `PodAssignment` at the entry write (rejected: it reverses CODE-4 item 4 and the two-releasers decision); a GC-side in-flight marker (rejected: a new §4.6.1 mechanism); a spec-stated ordering between `maxResumingSeconds` and `claimOrphanTimeout` (rejected: the two binaries cannot enforce it and the defaults are equal; the guidance goes in the flag help, CODE-4 item 19).
DECISION: The post-exit `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose` in `applyFailureFromResuming` (failure.go:318-320) is deleted — BECAUSE the next entry advances the generation, no binding carries `G` before the commit, and the spec requires the bump only on a mid-resume terminal collapse (§7.2 snapshot-close step 4(a); §4.2). F-7.1.14 stays closed through the terminal-write bump in session_transitions.go.
DECISION: SPEC-5i §7.2 delivery path 6 is the one home of message custody — BECAUSE the default inbox is per replica (cmd/lenny-gateway/session_messaging.go:100), no carrier exists (messages.go:149), and the DLQ take is atomic (dlq.go:123-135) — ALTERNATIVES: forward-then-enqueue on failure (double delivery on an ambiguous forward), lock-only ordering (does not reach other replicas), a sweep of `running` rows (a Redis call per running row per tick). This closes [spec.1.fix-G3.1] and [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1] for the `suspended` rows.
DECISION: Held-message delivery is level-triggered and at-least-once as SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** states. The retract-and-re-route of `holdMessage`, the stopped-drain restore, `PopOldest`, `RemoveAll`, `Restore`, the "a drain that completes" caveat, and the RECORDS-1 7(i) failed-re-read finding are deleted — BECAUSE no entry in a `running` DLQ waits past the next delivery point or past the idle suspension's backlog resume. The only race left is with a terminal transition, which the re-read with removal of the enqueuer's own entries covers, and a failed re-read removes the entries and answers `error` — ALTERNATIVES: a sweep of `running` rows (a Redis call per row per tick); a cross-replica wake (no carrier exists).
DECISION: The ledger OPEN "inbox-only `suspended` session never resumed" is closed. SPEC-5i **Backlog resume.** counts the inbox only for an `Idle` suspension and, for any other suspension reason, counts only the DLQ entries accepted after `SuspendedAt`, and no migration runs on entry to `suspended` — BECAUSE an inbox write classified before a suspend write can land after any migration, and the coordinating replica, to which the backlog resume is gated, holds the in-memory inbox. An interrupt suspension's inbox entries, and the DLQ entries a `running` period left, wait for the client, because §29.4 step 9 keeps the session `suspended` until the client acts or the suspended-session lifetime expires it, and SPEC-5b holds its pod so a client can decide. EVIDENCE: interrupt.go:86; mcptools_register.go:377, :553.
DECISION: The ledger OPEN "durable-inbox `EXPIRE` never removed" is closed. The durable `EXPIRE` on `resume_pending` is deleted (SPEC-5z item 6, CODE-4 item 18) — BECAUSE delivery after the commit or the terminal drain removes every entry, each recovering state is bounded, and a key `EXPIRE` deletes entries with no `message_expired`, including entries a `running` session buffered after the resume — ALTERNATIVES: a `PERSIST` on the commit to `running` (keeps an expiry whose only effect is silent loss). EVIDENCE: coordinator.go:108-119; sessioninbox.go:273; watchdog.go:906-918.
DECISION: SPEC-5m is a pointer at §6.2; SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** is the one home of the idle rule and its default. This closes [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1].
DECISION: The SPEC-5x carriers (schema enum, proto comment and generated binding, runtimeops.go and adapterclient comments) land in CODE-6 item 13, and the adapter-contract row lands in DOCS-1b. The schema-breaking gate does not inspect `$defs` enums.
DECISION: DOCS-1b mirrors SPEC-5w through state-machines.md's `suspended` row; SPEC-5y has no docs mirror.
DECISION: S1 names SPEC-1e and SPEC-1f, S2 names SPEC-2c's sites, and S7 reads "retry accounting".
DECISION: SPEC-1f also rewrites the §4.2 "first coordinator handoff ... mints 2" sentence and the §10.1.1 **Generation counters:** baseline — BECAUSE after the entry increment a session resumed before any takeover carries 2, and its first handoff mints 3 or more. EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:167; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:30.
DECISION: A `lenny/send_message` to a `suspended` target receives `queued` on both branches, and SPEC-5i states that receipt for that source — ALTERNATIVES: resume first and answer `delivered` on the MCP held-pod path (rejected: the MCP path then needs the synchronous resume-and-send that REST has, and a held-pod resume through `ResumeSuspended` carries no message of its own).
DECISION: When the zero-hold release clears `PodAssignment` between a message's read and `resumeHeldPod`, `resumeHeldPod` returns `errResumeHeldPodReleased` and the caller takes the pod-released branch (CODE-6 items 6 and 7) — BECAUSE the release takes no delivery lock, and leaving the row podless in `suspended` would delay the restore until the next suspended sweep's backlog resume. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/dlq.go:23 (`DefaultDLQTTL`).
DECISION: `ReattachNode` stops calling `resumeOnPod` — BECAUSE after CODE-4 item 4 only `completeResume` publishes a binding, so a reattach outside the driver would leave a pod claimed with no published binding. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/treerecovery.go:28-42, :72-78.
DECISION: `dlq_ttl_expired` keeps a state predicate naming the states `sweepDLQExpiry` covers (`resume_pending`, `resuming`, `awaiting_client_action`). SPEC-5z item 2 drops "and no resume occurred before the TTL boundary", and the §7.3 terminal-drain bullet (SPEC-5z item 1) covers every DLQ entry whatever the state in which it was enqueued — BECAUSE held-message delivery expires nothing, so only the sweep emits the reason, and the sweep does not expire a `suspended` session's held messages. The TTL runs from acceptance (dlq.go:95; SPEC-5z item 7), so an entry held longer than the TTL outside the recovering states expires at the first sweep after the session enters one, with `message_expired` to a sender session. This supersedes the round-2 CORRECTS and the state-free redefinition ([spec.2.review-mechanism.1] and its fixes) — ALTERNATIVES: the state-free definition (needed only while a stopped drain left entries in a `running` DLQ that a later drain expired); sweeping `suspended` rows (it races the backlog resume for an entry held since a `running` period); re-scoring held entries to the current time plus the TTL on entry to `resume_pending` (it breaks the acceptance order that delivery reads from the DLQ head). EVIDENCE: watchdog.go:684-707; coordinator.go:255-265.
DECISION: `clearInboxOnResume` is deleted — BECAUSE no resume clears an inbox, and §7.2 places `inbox_cleared` at lease takeover (RECORDS-1 7(h)). This closes the OPEN in [spec.2.fix-design-G2.1].
DECISION: Idle suspension is atomic with a turn through a synchronous admission write in `PodExecutor.Send` (`StampSync`, refusing a `suspended` row) and the `LastAgentActivityAt` guard on the `running → suspended` write, both serialized by the session row lock — BECAUSE inbound messages never stamp (sessionserver/events.go:362-370), `Stamp` is asynchronous and coalesced (sessionidle.go:183-191), `Send` has callers outside the message paths (mcptools_register.go:2636; translator/openai_chat.go:273; translator/open_responses.go:334), and the turn token is free between turns of one batch (podstream.go:184-209) — ALTERNATIVES: the delivery lock in `SuspendIdle` with `TryLock` (per replica, misses the callers outside the message paths), the turn token held across the write. This answers the UNVERIFIED in [spec.5.review-reliability.1].
DECISION: The Sweeper compares the row with the binding's own generation stamp, and `RecordHandoff` is the §10.1.2 step 1 compare-and-swap on the listed generation (CODE-5 items 2a and 2b) — BECAUSE the `renewedGeneration` map is cleared only when a sweep finds the session unbound or terminal (coordination.go:312, :335) and goes stale across a same-replica rebind, and an unconditional `RecordHandoff` (coordination.go:524-541) ends a live `resuming` incarnation — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the map and accepting the takeover-during-incarnation edge case.

### Open

OPEN: OD-1, the §6.2 **`resuming` timeout (300s):** bullet. Default: not rewritten; the guard is stated once in SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**, and the `UpdatedAt` anchor drift is an accepted edge case. Alternative: rewrite the bullet to state the last-update anchor, per-replica evaluation, and the incarnation guard as SPEC-1g, and reduce the SPEC-2a watchdog clause to a citation.
OPEN: OD-2, the `resuming → resume_pending` edge, which §6.2 states and the watchdog writes, is missing from the §7.2 state-machine block and from pkg/session/state/state.go. Default: not staged here; owned by the §7.2 state-machine area.
OPEN: OD-3, §7.2 snapshot-close step 1 says "Abort re-attach work immediately", while a terminal written on another replica reaches the attempt only at its next write. Default: no §7.2 edit; SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.** states when the attempt releases.
OPEN: OD-A, the cost of the per-`Send` admission write. Default: the synchronous `StampSync` write before the first envelope of every `PodExecutor.Send` (CODE-6 item 5), one guarded row write per message batch on the request path that covers every `Send` caller on every replica and records the §6.2 "a message" qualifying event. Alternative: `SuspendIdle` takes the delivery lock with `TryLock` and no admission write; it adds no store write but orders only the REST and MCP message paths on one replica, leaves the delegation child's first message and the single-shot translators unordered, and leaves the §6.2 message qualifying event unimplemented, which would become two RECORDS-1 findings.
OPEN: OD-B, the `errResumeHeldPodReleased → enterResumePending` branch. Default: keep it; `handleResume` needs it to answer `200` with `resume_pending`, and on the message paths it starts the restore at once. Alternative: drop it on the message paths only and leave the restore to the backlog resume, which saves a branch in `deliverMessageBatch` and `ResumeSuspended` and adds up to one watchdog tick of latency.
OPEN: OD-R1, delivery of a pre-running session's DLQ at `starting → running`. Default: the §7.2 pre-running dead-letter row stays as written ("The message is delivered when the session reaches `running`."), unbuilt as it is today; such an entry is delivered before the next path-2 message to the session, or through the idle suspension and the backlog resume. Alternative: add the commit from a pre-running state to the SPEC-5i **Delivery of held messages.** delivery points, point the row at that bullet, and add a `deliverBuffered` call on the `starting → running` path, which grows the proposal into the session-start path.
OPEN: OD-R2, the durable-inbox `EXPIRE` on `resume_pending`. Default: delete it (SPEC-5z item 6, CODE-4 item 18); a key held across a long recovering cycle grows only to `maxInboxSize`, and the terminal drain clears it. Alternative: keep the `EXPIRE` and add a `PERSIST` on every commit to `running`, which keeps a Redis memory bound for a session abandoned in recovery at the cost of silent loss for any entry still in the key when the TTL fires and one write per commit.

### Traps

WATCHOUT: Terminal writers read `fromState` outside their mutator and bump the generation in a second Update (session_transitions.go:35-50, :88-100). That is a §7.2 step 4 "same transaction" fidelity defect owned by the snapshot-close area. It does not affect exclusivity, because the commit requires `resuming`.
WATCHOUT: `resume_rebind.go` stamps the binding with the generation of the row read before entry. After the entry increment, that value is `G−1`, and `reportSupersededByPeer` drops every stream failure of a binding below the row's generation (stream_failure.go:115). CODE-4 item 5.4 restamps with `G`.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce "inbox buffering" for any `suspended`-target fallback in SPEC-5i, SPEC-5l, SPEC-5v, or the §29.3 lead; the `running` and `input_required` inbox fallback is RECORDS-1 item 7(f), deliberately unstaged.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the SPEC-1b condition that each attempt write sees the state the attempt last read or wrote and an unchanged `coordination_generation`. It lost because it is ABA-prone: the watchdog exit bumps nothing, so a re-entry by another attempt restores the (state, generation) pair and a stale attempt's commit passes. Do not restore it; the entry increment plus the incarnation condition on every exit replaces it.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: an `UpdatedAt` equality guard on the `sweepResuming` exit, and `UpdatedAt` as the attempt token. The guard was ABA-safe but lost so that every `resuming` exit uses the one generation condition and no Postgres microsecond round-trip test is needed. The token lost because the asynchronous `AdvanceLastSeq` write after every event Publish advances `UpdatedAt` before the commit.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the post-exit `bumpCoordinationGenerationOnSnapshotClose` call in `applyFailureFromResuming`, and the SPEC-1b claims that the driver runs at most one attempt per session and that an attempt ends by the `resuming` watchdog's deadline. The bump lost because the next entry increments the generation and the spec requires a bump only on a mid-resume terminal collapse. The claims lost because both are false (two replicas claim before the entry write; the watchdog anchor moves with every row write) and no rule depends on them.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the replica-local session-inbox fallback for a `suspended` target (SPEC-5i, the §29.3 step 1 text and lead, `bufferTargetInbox` after a held-pod resume fault, and the MCP `lenny/send_message` executor path), and `ReattachNode` claiming a pod through `resumeOnPod`. The inbox lost because the default `MemoryInbox` is per replica and the coordinating replica never reads it; the DLQ is a shared Redis key. The reattach claim lost because only `completeResume` publishes a binding, so a claim outside the driver leaves a claimed pod with no binding.
WATCHOUT: Do not restore a forward, an inbox fallback, or an inbox write in `deliverBuffered` for a `suspended` target. Do not call `ResumeSuspended` while holding the delivery lock. Do not take the delivery lock in `SuspendIdle`, and do not route the delivery admission through the asynchronous `Stamp`.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the off-holder forward of a message for a held-pod `suspended` session to the coordinator, with its unreachable-coordinator DLQ fallback and the `ForwardMessage` reference in SPEC-5i. It lost because no inter-replica carrier exists and a forward with an ambiguous outcome followed by an enqueue delivers twice; every message not delivered synchronously is held in the DLQ and the coordinating replica's backlog resume delivers it.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: `DrainForResume` feeding drained messages back through the §7.2 classifier, which buffered them in the inbox while a turn was in flight, and `clearInboxOnResume` on every resume commit. The drain lost because an inbox write after a commit can outlive the drain, and `DLQ.DrainAll` takes every entry at once so a failed send loses the rest; `deliverBuffered` peeks the older of the inbox and DLQ heads and removes an entry only after its delivery. `clearInboxOnResume` lost because no resume clears an inbox.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: 0091's per-session `renewedGeneration` record read by `peerTookOver`, and the unconditional `RecordHandoff` increment. The record lost because only an unbound or terminal sweep clears it, so a same-replica rebind is compared against the earlier binding's value and a live binding is evicted. The unconditional increment lost because a Sweeper that listed `resume_pending` before the driver's entry write ends the live `resuming` incarnation.
WATCHOUT: Replaced: idle-suspension atomicity from `TurnInFlight` and the `LastAgentActivityAt` guard alone, and the proposed `TryLock` of the delivery lock in `SuspendIdle`. The guard alone lost because no delivery stamps `LastAgentActivityAt` and `Stamp` is asynchronous; the turn token is free between turns of one batch; and the lock is per replica and misses the `Send` callers outside the message paths.
WATCHOUT: Do not restore retract-after-drain, a restore of attempted DLQ entries, expiry inside the delivery loop, the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows, the durable inbox `EXPIRE`, an inbox-to-DLQ migration on entry to `suspended`, or a backlog resume keyed on the inbox of an interrupt suspension or on its DLQ entries accepted before the suspension.
WATCHOUT: Do not refile "the orphan GC can drain a pod mid-restore". It is the accepted outcome stated in SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**, and the GC's lookup-then-drain race predates this proposal (gc.go:233-244).
WATCHOUT: Replaced: the drain after a commit to `running` (SPEC-5i **Delivery after a commit to `running`.** and **Enqueue after a drain.**), with `holdMessage`'s retract-and-re-route, `DLQ.PopOldest`, `DLQ.RemoveAll`, `DLQ.Restore`, expiry inside the drain, the DLQ sweep of `suspended` rows, the state-free `dlq_ttl_expired` definition, and the `queued` answer on a failed re-read. It lost because a stopped drain left entries in a `running` DLQ that nothing delivered until the next commit, the retract raced the drain and needed an all-or-nothing removal, and the sweep of `suspended` rows raced the backlog resume. Delivery of held messages runs at every commit and before every path-2 delivery, removes an entry only after its `Send`, and expires nothing.

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `resuming` is not adopted; SPEC-2a states that every replica evaluates the §6.2 `resuming` watchdog from the session row — BECAUSE the watchdog is a per-replica row scan in the tree and keeping `resuming` out of adoption keeps `coordination_generation` fixed during a restore — ALTERNATIVES: adopting `resuming` (the adopter would inherit a half-claimed pod, and the generation would change mid-restore).
FACT: The §6.2 `resuming` watchdog exits to `resume_pending` or to `awaiting_client_action` when retries are exhausted. The SPEC-2a sentence names both exits, which differs from the design's wording that named only `resume_pending`. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:764 sweepResuming; cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go:221 per-replica watchdog.New.
DECISION: SPEC-2a states lease renewal for "every non-terminal state except `created`, `finalizing`, and `ready`" and cites §29.3 for why those states have no holder. "After the first bind" and "never been bound" are removed — BECAUSE finalize binds a pod (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:573) and the spec states no point where the lease is first acquired (:569) — ALTERNATIVES: "after the lease is first acquired" (rejected because it names an event the spec does not define).
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce "fenced at bind" or a CoordinatorFence on the driver's newly claimed pod. No stale coordinator ever addresses that pod. The split-brain is in the row writes, and a generation condition closes it: the SPEC-1b preamble requires each attempt write to see an unchanged `coordination_generation`, and CODE-4 items 3.4 and 5.2 implement that. §10.1.2 step 3 already accepts RPCs on a pod with no fenced generation. EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:41.
FACT: `acquireCoordinationLease` (the CODE-4 item 3.3 self-acquire) does not bump `coordination_generation`. Only the Sweeper's `RecordHandoff` and the snapshot-close bump do, so the generation read at item 3.1 stays valid through the driver's own lease acquire. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/coordination_lease.go:34-45; pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:524; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/session_generation.go:39.
DECISION: The commit guard (item 5.2) carries the generation condition as well as the hook (item 3.4) — BECAUSE a stale driver can see the watchdog return its row to `resume_pending`, then a peer adopt it, then the peer's driver write `resuming` again, and after that a commit guarded on state alone would pass — ALTERNATIVES: guarding the `resuming` write only.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The resume driver's exclusive edge is `resume_pending → resuming`, not every exit from `resume_pending` — BECAUSE the pre-attach collapse (§7.2), the window cap to `awaiting_client_action` (§6.2/§7.3 step 3c), and §8.10 tree recovery all write other exits — ALTERNATIVES: listing those exceptions in SPEC-1b (rejected; restates edges §7.2 owns).
DECISION: SPEC-1e keys the §7.2 pre-attach collapse on the `resume_pending → resuming` write rather than on the claim — BECAUSE the driver claims first (decision 2), so a claimed-but-pending window exists; the driver releases its claim per SPEC-1b item 2 — ALTERNATIVES: writing `resuming` before the claim (reverses decision 2 and the 0091 finding), a claimed-but-pending sub-state (new state edge).
FACT: §7.2 sites that keyed the collapse on the claim are the `resume_pending → cancelled` and `→ completed` state-block lines, the **Pre-attach terminal collapse** paragraph, and its **No snapshot-close sequence.** bullet — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:200, 201, 226, 227. spec/06:179 and spec/06:243 ("half-claimed replacement pod" on resuming → cancelled) stay true.
DECISION: Deleted "`retryPolicy.maxRetries` is the only retry budget" everywhere — BECAUSE `sessionPolicy.slotRetries` budgets the same pod-crash event on concurrent pools — EVIDENCE: spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:598. ALTERNATIVES: "only session-level budget" (how slotRetries composes with retryCount is unstated; next finding).
OPEN: How `sessionPolicy.slotRetries` and `retryPolicy.maxRetries` compose on a concurrent-pod crash that fails active slots is unstated in spec and proposal.
WATCHOUT: SPEC-3g's "stays in resume_pending when the refusal arrives before the claim" branch was unreachable (a slot-bind refusal comes from the claimed pod, after the `resuming` write) and is deleted; SPEC-3h's pin-read branch is pre-claim and reachable, keep it — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:734.
DECISION: §29.6 keeps the podless `suspended` /resume as an untraced entry point (SPEC-3k unchanged); SPEC-3m now says every `suspended` call leaves the trace; Preconditions stay `awaiting_client_action` — BECAUSE widening Preconditions would falsify untouched steps 1 and 3 (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:1040-1060).
DECISION: SPEC-3o is now a pointer to §7.3 **Resume driver.** items 1 and 2 — BECAUSE items 1-2 are the single home of the claim-failure rule and the write ordering.

### [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: The §6.2 **Idle suspension.** paragraph (SPEC-5f) is the one home of the idle firing rule and of the "no turn in flight" gloss; the SPEC-5e `running` clock row is a pointer and the SPEC-5f lead sentence "When the clock fires..." is deleted — BECAUSE the paragraph states the action (write, reason, release, resume) — ALTERNATIVES: defining the term in the table cell (rejected: normative paragraph would depend on a cell).
DECISION: New SPEC-5 items for §8.8 (SPEC-5w), §28.5.3 (SPEC-5x), §29.4 (SPEC-5y) sit before the sweep, which keeps its label SPEC-5u and its letter-independent "After the other SPEC-5 edits" (set by an earlier group). ### [spec.1.fix-G5.1]
DECISION: The root's budget-key TTL is re-armed on both entry to and exit from `suspended` (SPEC-5o is the single home), and the absolute "must never fire" sentence is deleted — BECAUSE a leave-only re-arm leaves a suspension only TTL minus prior unsuspended time, so a root could meet BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED mid-suspension — ALTERNATIVES: sliding TTL on every budget_reserve/budget_return call (changes the Lua contract and still expires idle trees); entry-only re-arm (the cycling awaiting_client_action/resume_pending case keeps only about 1 d of margin); re-arm on every transition (more Redis writes for no further guarantee).
WATCHOUT: Do not restore the §6.2 sentence "if the budget key TTL fires while the session is still suspended ... transitions to `failed`" that SPEC-5g deletes. The `suspended → failed (BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED)` rows and §8.3 cleanup carry that outcome. EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:216, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:194, spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408-410
FACT: §17.8.1 carries a separate `Delegation budget key TTL` row (259200 s) that is apart from §8.3 and §11.3. SPEC-5s now replaces its value with 691200 s (8 d). The only budget-key TTL sites in spec are 08:406, 11 (SPEC-5n row), and 17:932. EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:932

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: `resuming` is not adopted; SPEC-2a gains one sentence pointing at the §6.2 `resuming` watchdog evaluated from the session row — BECAUSE excluding it keeps `coordination_generation` fixed for the whole of a driver's `resuming` window (only adoption and the terminal collapse bump it), which makes a generation condition on the driver's row writes sufficient fencing — ALTERNATIVES: adopting `resuming` (adopter inherits a half-claimed pod held by a dead attempt, and the generation can change mid-restore, so the commit needs more than a guard).
DECISION: SPEC-1b fencing is a generation condition on every driver row write (the `resuming` write and the `running` commit), stated once in the attempt preamble; no fence-at-claim is added — BECAUSE the replacement pod was never addressed by another coordinator, and §10.1.2 step 3 already lets a pod with no fenced generation accept the session's RPCs — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "send CoordinatorFence before the first operational RPC" (unneeded once the writes are conditional, and conflicts with CODE-4 item 4 / summary Watch-out on fencing after the commit).
WATCHOUT: the guard on the `resuming` write alone is not enough. A stale driver A in `resuming` with a lapsed lease can have its row returned to `resume_pending` by the watchdog, adopted by B, and moved to `resuming` again by B's driver; A's commit guarded only on `State == resuming` then passes. The commit needs the generation condition too — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5.2 (state-only guard); pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go sweepResuming (row scan, RetryCount++).
FACT: the session watchdog runs on every replica (constructed in cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go, not in leader_sweeps.go) and `sweepResuming` is a row scan anchored on `UpdatedAt`, so a `resuming` row recovers after its coordinator dies — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/controlserver.go:221; pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:764-791.
FACT: the §29.3 terminate row is the spec statement of which non-terminal states have a coordinating replica (`starting`, `running`, `suspended`, `resume_pending`, `awaiting_client_action`) and that `created`/`finalizing`/`ready` have none; SPEC-2a should cite it instead of claiming those states were "never bound" (finalize resolves a bound pod) — EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.3 off-holder table, terminate and finalize rows.
WATCHOUT: summary.md rejected-alternatives line "A generation-guarded commit with publish-after-commit plus a lock..." reads as rejecting the commit guard this design adds; reword it in the same edit.
OPEN: CODE-4 item 3.3 lets a driver take a lapsed lease with a plain `acquireCoordinationLease` and no `RecordHandoff` when both A's and B's leases lapsed; the attempt's generation read at item 3.1 then passes. Narrow, pre-existing in the draft, not filed.
WATCHOUT: a pod claimed by a driver whose replica died in `resuming` is released by no stated actor in this proposal; pre-existing orphan-claim behaviour, not this group's scope.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: §29.6 podless-suspended /resume stays an untraced entry point named by the intro (SPEC-3k); SPEC-3m sends every `suspended` call out of the trace — BECAUSE widening the Preconditions also falsifies tree step 1 ("explicit resume available once automatic retries are exhausted") and step 3 ("writes the `awaiting_client_action → resume_pending` transition"), which no staged edit touches — ALTERNATIVES: widen SPEC-3l plus edit steps 1 and 3 (three more staged edits for the same restore steps).
DECISION: the claim-before-`resuming` window is closed by a new SPEC-1e that keys the §7.2 pre-attach collapse on the `resume_pending → resuming` write and points the "half-claimed pod" bullet at §7.3 **Resume driver.** item 2 — BECAUSE decision 2 (claim first) is settled and 0091 filed resuming-before-claim as a defect — ALTERNATIVES: write `resuming` before the claim (reverses decision 2).
DECISION: "only retry budget" is deleted rather than qualified ("only session budget") — BECAUSE §6.2 and §5.2 apply `sessionPolicy.slotRetries` to the same pod-crash event and the qualifier would itself be disputed; SPEC-1c and the §7.2 edges already state `retryCount < maxRetries`.
FACT: §7.2 keys the pre-attach collapse on "before a replacement pod is claimed" in three places: both `resume_pending → {cancelled,completed}` state-block lines and the **Pre-attach terminal collapse** paragraph plus its **No snapshot-close sequence.** bullet ("no half-claimed replacement pod to release") — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:200,201,226,227
OPEN: how `slotRetries` and `retryCount`/`retryPolicy.maxRetries` compose when a concurrent pod crash fails active slots (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:183, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:598) is unstated in the spec and in this proposal; a later round may file it.
WATCHOUT: G1 adds a generation guard to driver items 2-3; SPEC-3o's pointer cites items 1 and 2 by number, so G1 must not renumber those items.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S7 says "Lands the single retry budget"; after this round CODE-2 is "Retry accounting" and no "single budget" is claimed — S7 should read "Lands the retry accounting with its tests". S1 does not name the new SPEC-1e §7.2 pre-attach edit; it should name it with the §7.3 driver.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: ownership split for suspended-session routing — §7.2 delivery path 6 (SPEC-5i) owns the state transitions for all three triggers (message, resume_session, POST /resume) and the message off-holder rule; the §29.3 matrix owns the off-holder outcome of the POST /resume rows, with owner cell §29 on all three /resume rows — BECAUSE the §29 intro makes the matrix normative for every non-message route and the fail-closed rule for forwarded non-message rows lives in the §29.3 lead paragraph — ALTERNATIVES: owner §7.2 on the podless /resume row (finding 1's suggestion) rejected, since §7.2 states no off-holder rule for /resume and would need a new fail-closed sentence to cover the held-pod row.
DECISION: an off-holder podless message goes to the session DLQ (dead-letter table recovering row) in both durableInbox modes — BECAUSE staged CODE-6 item 7 already calls bufferTargetDLQ and CODE-4 DrainForResume reads the DLQ after durable-inbox entries — ALTERNATIVES: "Redis inbox under durableInbox: true" (finding 2's suggestion) rejected, since it diverges from the staged code.
FACT: cross-replica ForwardMessage is ABSENT in the tree; §28.5.4 records the ABSENT claim row. Today messages_delivery.go runs resumeHeldPod locally on whichever replica serves the call. EVIDENCE: spec/28_communication-channels.md §28.5.4 last paragraph; pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messages_delivery.go ActionResumeAndDeliver case.
FACT: enterResumePending (staged CODE-4 item 9) calls migrateInboxOnResumePending on the serving replica, so the spec needs no sentence about draining the coordinator's inbox for the podless transition. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 9.
OPEN: The live §29.3 lead and the held-pod message row both say an unreachable coordinator makes the serving replica "fall back to inbox buffering". That inbox belongs to the coordinator under durableInbox: false, which is the same defect as finding 2 for the held-pod branch. This defect predates the proposal, so this group left it alone. File it in a later round. EVIDENCE: spec/29_communication-scenarios.md §29.3 Off-holder matrix lead ("a message send falls back to inbox buffering"); spec/07_session-lifecycle.md path 6.
WATCHOUT: §7.2's inbox-to-DLQ migration paragraph says the drain runs "within the same goroutine that executes the resume_pending state transition". Once any replica writes the podless transition, that sentence no longer holds where the coordinator's in-memory inbox is concerned. It is outside this group's findings. Do not fix it by restating the drain inside path 6.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: The SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** paragraph is the one home of the idle firing rule and of the "no turn in flight" gloss; the §6.2 idle-table `running` row and the SPEC-5f replacement sentence become pointers, and the SPEC-5e intro drops its "releasing the session's pod or slot" clause — BECAUSE single-source — ALTERNATIVES: keeping the definition in the table row (the paragraph is where the action is stated, so the term belongs there).
DECISION: A zero-hold (or any-hold) release clears the pod binding only with a write conditioned on `suspended` + the same pod; on a failed condition nothing is released. Staged in SPEC-5b step 2 — BECAUSE CODE-6 item 4.3 already builds exactly this guard (`State == suspended && PodAssignment == row.PodAssignment`), so the spec was the only site missing it — ALTERNATIVES: serialising release and held-pod resume on the coordinator in-process (not a spec-level guarantee; the store guard is what both sides already use).
DECISION: §28.5.3 `deadline_approaching` loses the `"idle"` trigger value rather than being re-described as a pre-suspension warning — BECAUSE idle suspension fires only with no turn in flight, so there is no work to wrap up, nothing in pkg/ sends it (expiry_warning.go sends only session_age), and a re-description would need a conditional in a row that says "forced termination" — ALTERNATIVES: keep `idle` with "or before idle suspension" (spec obligation with no sender, hair).
DECISION: New SPEC-5 items (§29.4, §8.8, §28.5.3) go before the bounded sweep, and the sweep's "After SPEC-5a to SPEC-5t" becomes "After the other SPEC-5 edits" so it never needs a letter range again. SPEC-5u has no citers outside itself.
FACT: Globbing `*spec-changes.md` in the proposal dir also matches `*non-spec-changes.md` (sorted first), so `awk NR` line numbers are off by 270. Use the full filename. EVIDENCE: proposal dir listing.
FACT: `"idle"` deadline trigger appears only at spec/28_communication-channels.md (deadline_approaching row), docs/reference/adapter-contract.md (deadline_approaching row), and the doc comment of `SignalDeadlineApproaching` in pkg/adapter/runtimeops.go; no schema enum, no sender, no conformance case. 0090 spec-changes noted `trigger: idle` but did not decide on it.
OPEN: SPEC-5m (§5.2 **`maxClientIdleSeconds`** paragraph) restates the idle predicate ("while its runtime has no turn in flight ... releases the session's pod or slot at once") that SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** owns; a later single-source round should cut it to a pointer.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: the SPEC-5o re-arm fires when a root with a delegation tree enters `suspended` as well as when it leaves. Because the TTL is re-armed to its full value at entry and has to exceed `gateway.maxSuspendedSessionSeconds`, it cannot run out during a suspension that started with a successful re-arm. SPEC-5o is the one home for the re-arm rule and for the residual (a root out of `suspended` longer than the TTL meets BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED). SPEC-5g and the Edge-cases bullet point to it. BECAUSE re-arming only on leave left TTL minus the non-suspended time since the last arm at suspension entry, and that can fall below the 7 d lifetime. ALTERNATIVES: a sliding TTL refreshed by budget_reserve/return.lua (rejected: a tree with no delegation operations for longer than the TTL still expires, and it changes the Lua contract); re-arm on entry only (rejected: the cycling awaiting_client_action/resume_pending case would get about 1 d instead of 8 d); re-arm on every state transition (rejected: more Redis writes for no extra guarantee).
FACT: the observable outcome of a TTL expiry (root goes to `failed`) is already stated in §8.3 **`BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` detection and tree cleanup.** item 3 and in the §6.2/§7.2 `suspended → failed` state-machine rows, so the §6.2 sentence that SPEC-5g deletes does not need restoring. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:414, spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:216
FACT: `259200` budget-key TTL default appears in exactly three spec sites: §8.3 :406 (SPEC-5o), §11.3 :240 (SPEC-5n), and §17.8.1 :932 (was unstaged; now SPEC-5s). EVIDENCE: grep -rn 259200 spec/
OPEN: SPEC-5o re-arms only the root's tree-wide budget keys, while §8.3 sets the TTL on "all delegation budget keys" (per-child keys included). If a per-child key expires while the tree-wide sentinel survives, the expiry goes undetected. This was not adjudicated here; a later round should check whether the re-arm must cover the per-child keys.

FACT: The stale attempt needs no separate pod release on this path, because the binder's `failResume` releases the claim on a post-RPC failure before the error returns. The SPEC-1b preamble "releases the pod it claimed" therefore holds without new code. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/podlifecycle/podsession/resume.go:168, :274

### [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: Writers that move a session out of `resume_pending` besides the driver exist in the spec: §7.2 pre-attach terminal collapse (`resume_pending → cancelled|completed`), §7.3 step 3c / §6.2 `resume_pending` wall-clock cap (`→ awaiting_client_action`), and §8.10 tree recovery (level/tree timeout marks nodes failed or expired). FACT: The default-mode session inbox is in-memory on the coordinating replica, and the inbox-to-DLQ drain runs in the goroutine that writes `resume_pending`; a non-coordinator writing a podless `suspended → resume_pending` cannot hold or drain that inbox. Recovering-state messages go to the Redis DLQ, which any replica can write. — EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:283-289, 314-320, 350
FACT: No spec section defines a fence at bind; CH-FENCE is announced only on coordinator handoff, and the pod holds no generation for a session until its first fence. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:38-41, spec/28_communication-channels.md:335-345
FACT: `sessionPolicy.slotRetries` is a second recovery budget applied to pod failure with active slots on concurrent pods. — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:183, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:598
WATCHOUT: §17.8.1 carries a "Delegation budget key TTL" row (259200 s) separate from the idle rows SPEC-5s edits; §29.4 steps 9-10 still say the idle clock fires `expired` and a podless suspended session waits "until the client acts". — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:932, spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:686-702
WATCHOUT: Slot-bind refusals are pod-registry refusals, so they arrive only after the claim and (per SPEC-1b item 2) after `resuming` is written; a "before the replacement pod is claimed" branch is unreachable. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:734
OPEN: Budget-key TTL re-arm only on leaving `suspended` plus "TTL must exceed lifetime" does not stop the TTL firing during a later suspension when non-suspended time since the last re-arm exceeds TTL − lifetime; re-arming on entry to `suspended` closes it.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: Top-tier capacity numbers for lease math: Tier 3 = 10,000 concurrent sessions, 200 creations/s (spec/16_observability.md:595-597); lease renewal row ~1,000/s, total Redis writes ~6,500/s (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:266-268); Coordination Redis role described as "low throughput, small key space" (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:253); Postgres sustained write IOPS ~1,300/s, burst ~3,900/s at Tier 3 (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:70, spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1176-1177); REG-COORDLEASE TTL 60 s (spec/28_communication-channels.md:137). EVIDENCE: as cited.
FACT: In the shipped tree no podless suspended row exists and every non-terminal row is expired 7200 s after creation (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:1084 `now.Sub(row.CreatedAt)`), and the coordination Sweeper never adopts a suspended session (pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:262 isRunningPod). So SPEC-2a + SPEC-5g create a new population of 7-day podless suspended sessions that hold and renew REG-COORDLEASE. EVIDENCE: as cited.
WATCHOUT: The Sweeper upserts the REG-COORDMIRROR Postgres row for every held lease on every sweep (coordination.go:503 upsertMirror), so every lease the spec makes a replica hold is also a periodic Postgres write in the tree. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/coordination/coordination/coordination.go:503,632.
DECISION: Filed one finding: SPEC-2a lease retention in podless `suspended` scales Redis renewals, Postgres-fallback renewals, and crash/rolling-update adoptions with the 7-day suspended population — BECAUSE abandoned sessions now live 604800 s rather than 7200 s — ALTERNATIVES: filing checkpoint/MinIO load of idle suspension (rejected: shipped sessions already export a final snapshot and pods are held no longer than under shipped idle expiry), resume-driver retry cadence (rejected: claim rate is bounded by client resumes, speculative).

DECISION: security lens filed no findings on the spec staging — BECAUSE the controls it touches stay intact: pin-read failure on resume still claims no pod (SPEC-3h), BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED still fails the tree closed (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:408-417, not edited), cross-tenant send validation covers all message paths (spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:277), and the idle release takes the pool's session-end disposition (recycle runs credential purge and whole-pod scrub, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:480) — ALTERNATIVES: filing "no turn in flight" as a pod self-report was rejected because the idle bound is resource reclamation and maxSessionAge still caps a pod that never ends its turn; "any message resumes, including a sibling's queued send" is an owner decision.

### [spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: §7.3 **Resume driver.** item 4 (SPEC-1b) is the single home of inbox delivery on resume: session-inbox entries first, then recovery-time (DLQ) entries, FIFO, before post-transition messages; §29.6 step 14 (SPEC-3p) now points at item 4; CODE-4 item 6 `DrainForResume` reads the coordinator's inbox in both `messaging.durableInbox` modes — BECAUSE the driver runs on the coordinating replica, which holds the default-mode in-memory inbox, and a non-coordinator serving the podless `suspended → resume_pending` write cannot drain it — ALTERNATIVES: drain inbox to DLQ at pod release (DLQ entries score at now + maxResumeWindowSeconds and would expire during a 7-day suspension); restate the drain in path 6 / SPEC-5c (serving replica cannot reach the in-memory inbox; second home); forward to coordinator (reintroduces ABSENT ForwardMessage).
WATCHOUT: `clearInboxOnResume` → `ClearInboxOnAcquire` clears nothing; it only emits `inbox_cleared`. Do not reorder it around `deliverBuffered`. A separate pre-existing defect: in default mode it emits reason `coordinator_failover` on every restore. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go ClearInboxOnAcquire.
FACT: `sessioninbox.Coordinator.inbox` is MemoryInbox by default and RedisInbox in durable mode, so reading it covers both modes. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go Coordinator/Config.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce "held in the session inbox" for the triggering podless message; it still goes to the DLQ. Only pre-suspension entries sit in the inbox. Default-mode crash loss of those entries is within the documented §7.2 guarantee.

### [spec.2.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5o re-arms the TTL of every budget key of the root's tree (tree-wide and per-parent) — BECAUSE §8.3 Defense-in-depth TTL sets the TTL on all delegation budget keys, and the BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED sentinel check reads only `{root}:dlg:tokens`, so a per-parent key expiring before the sentinel silently resets childrenTotal — ALTERNATIVES: extend the sentinel check to per-parent keys (changes landed §8.3 contract); exempt per-parent keys from TTL (contradicts §8.3).
FACT: §8.3 lists tree-wide keys (tokens, tree_size, tree_memory) and per-parent keys (parallel_children:{parent}, children_total:{parent}); the sentinel check is on `{root}:dlg:tokens` only — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md "Tree-wide keys" / "Per-parent keys" bullets and **`BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED` detection and tree cleanup.**
WATCHOUT: the re-arm implementation must enumerate the per-parent keys of every delegating parent in the tree, not only the root's own; that enumeration is RECORDS-1's concern and was deliberately left out of the staged text.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: Pre-suspension inbox entries are delivered by the resume driver, which runs on the coordinating replica that holds the default-mode in-memory inbox. SPEC-1b item 4 (§7.3 **Resume driver.**) is the one home: it delivers session-inbox entries, then the entries buffered while recovering. CODE-4 item 6 `DrainForResume` reads the session inbox through `Coordinator.inbox` in both modes, not only the durable inbox — BECAUSE the coordinator keeps the in-memory inbox through a podless suspension, and `Coordinator.inbox` is the in-memory store or the Redis list depending on the mode — ALTERNATIVES: draining the inbox to the DLQ when the pod is released (SPEC-5b step 2) was rejected because DLQ entries are scored by expiry at now + maxResumeWindowSeconds and would expire during a suspension that can last 7 days. Restoring the §7.2 inbox-to-DLQ drain inside path 6 was rejected because the serving replica cannot reach the coordinator's inbox (see the [spec.1.fix-G3.1] WATCHOUT).
FACT: FIFO holds in both serving cases. When the coordinator serves the podless transition, `enterResumePending` → `migrateInboxOnResumePending` moves the inbox into the DLQ before CODE-6 item 7's `bufferTargetDLQ`, so the DLQ score order is already FIFO. When a non-coordinator serves it, that replica's drain is a no-op, and the coordinator's inbox entries, which are older, come before the DLQ. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/messaging.go:112; pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go:104 (MigrateInboxToDLQ drains c.inbox and enqueues to the DLQ with DefaultDLQTTL); non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 9, CODE-6 item 7.
DECISION: SPEC-5b gains no "release leaves the inbox in place" sentence. Nothing states that the release clears the inbox. In default mode, a coordinator crash during suspension or recovery loses the in-memory inbox under the existing guarantee at spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:314 ("does not drop ... while the coordinating replica remains alive").
FACT: Despite its name, `clearInboxOnResume` / `ClearInboxOnAcquire` clears nothing. It emits only `inbox_cleared` with the DLQ length. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/coordinator.go:232-248.
OPEN: CODE-4 item 5.5 moves `clearInboxOnResume` into the driver's tail unchanged. In default mode the driver then emits `inbox_cleared` with reason `coordinator_failover` on every restore, including restores where no failover happened and the driver is about to deliver the inbox. This existed before the move, in handleResume, and is a separate finding for a later round.

### [spec.2.fix-design-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5o re-arm scope becomes "every budget key of that root's tree" (replace in place) — BECAUSE §8.3 Defense-in-depth TTL puts the TTL on all delegation budget keys, the per-parent keys are not checked by the sentinel test, and a per-parent key expiring under a live sentinel silently resets childrenTotal — ALTERNATIVES: making the Lua scripts check per-parent key existence (new mechanism, changes landed §8.3 sentinel contract); dropping TTL on per-parent keys (contradicts §8.3 "all delegation budget keys").
WATCHOUT: the re-arm must enumerate per-parent keys `{root}:dlg:parallel_children:{parent}` and `{root}:dlg:children_total:{parent}`; the gateway knows the delegating parents from the session tree rows, so no pattern SCAN is needed. The re-arm has no implementation in this proposal (RECORDS-1 files it), so this is a note for that later builder — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md "Per-parent keys" bullet in §8.3; non-spec-changes.md item 11 and RECORDS-1 (d).
FACT: the only proposal site naming the re-arm's key scope is spec-changes.md SPEC-5o; spec-changes.md line 24 and the §6.2 trigger-mechanism text in SPEC-5 point to §8.3 without naming the key set — EVIDENCE: grep "tree-wide|budget key" over the proposal files.

### [spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The live §7.2 states twice that a suspended session's inbox survives to the resume: path 6's podless bullet ("the standard `resume_pending` inbox handling applies (inbox-to-DLQ drain ...)") and the paragraph after it ("all buffered inbox messages are delivered in FIFO order"). The §6.2 "Once the pod is released" paragraph also states it. This round's SPEC-5c and SPEC-5i rewrites delete all three. The generic §7.2 drain rule is scoped "(due to pod failure or eviction)" and runs in the goroutine that writes the transition, so a non-coordinator serving replica cannot reach the coordinator's in-memory inbox. EVIDENCE: spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:314, :320, :335, :337; spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:228.
WATCHOUT: Inbox content can exist in a podless `suspended` session even though any message now resumes it. Messages queued with `delivery: queued` while a turn was in flight are still in the inbox when an interrupt suspends the session, because the runtime never re-enters `ready_for_input`. SPEC-1b item 4 delivers only "messages buffered while the session was recovering", and CODE-4 `DrainForResume` reads only the durable inbox and the DLQ. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 6.
FACT: The resuming watchdog defaults to 300 s (pkg/gateway/runtime/watchdog/watchdog.go:155), which equals claimOrphanTimeout's 5-minute default (spec/04_system-components.md:517). CODE-4 defers PodAssignment until the commit, so the driver's claimed pod is unreferenced for up to the whole `resuming` window. Today the watchdog cancels the attempt before the orphan GC can drain it. Raising MaxResumingSeconds above claimOrphanTimeout would let the GC drain a pod mid-restore. Not filed, because it is a code choice.

### [spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1b's attempt write condition is the row's state as the attempt last read or wrote it plus an unchanged `coordination_generation`, with at most one attempt per session and each attempt ending by the `resuming` watchdog's deadline — BECAUSE the §6.2 `resuming` watchdog returns the row to `resume_pending` without bumping the generation (spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241; only adoption and snapshot-close bump), so a generation-only guard let an overtaken attempt and a fresh one both commit — ALTERNATIVES: a watchdog exception clause (each later overtaking write needs another), a state-only CAS (ABA when attempt B rewrites `resuming`), having the watchdog bump the generation (changes landed 0091 semantics), copying CODE-4's in-flight set and timer into the spec (mechanism belongs to CODE-4).
WATCHOUT: SPEC-1b item 2 no longer carries its own "left `resume_pending` by then, release and stop" sentence; the lead write condition covers it. Do not reinstate it, and do not renumber items 1-4 (SPEC-3o cites items 1 and 2, SPEC-3p cites item 4). Pointers that named "item 2" for the release (SPEC-1e item 4, edge case **Terminate or cancel racing the driver.**) now name the paragraph.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1]: the WATCHOUT there says the generation condition alone closes the split-brain; the watchdog-overtaken same-generation overlap also needs the state condition and the one-attempt bound, now both in SPEC-1b.
FACT: §8.10 Delegation Tree Recovery owns the bottom-up traversal; SPEC-3j row 1 now cites §8.10 beside §7.3. No SPEC-1b item states a traversal and none should. EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:1018.

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1b's write condition becomes a compare-and-swap on the state the attempt last read or wrote plus the unchanged coordination_generation, with "at most one attempt per session at a time, ending no later than the `resuming` watchdog's deadline" — BECAUSE the watchdog moves `resuming → resume_pending` without a generation bump, and a state-only CAS has an ABA hole (B rewrites `resuming` before A commits) that only the one-attempt bound closes — ALTERNATIVES: generation-only guard (the failed predicate); state-only CAS (ABA); bump the generation on the watchdog write (changes §6.2 and the 0091 generation semantics for a non-adoption edge).
DECISION: item 2's "When the session row has left `resume_pending` by then, the driver releases the claimed pod and stops" is deleted as a restatement of the lead condition; SPEC-1e item 4 and the **Terminate or cancel racing the driver.** edge case retarget from "item 2" to **Resume driver.**; SPEC-3o "items 1 and 2" stays (item numbering unchanged).
FACT: CODE-4 already implements the new condition: in-flight set (item 2), deadline reset to MaxResumingSeconds (item 3.4), hook guard State==resume_pending+gen (3.4), commit guard State==resuming+gen with pod release (5.2-5.3), failure report gen-guarded and routed by state (3.7). No non-spec change needed — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 items 2, 3.4, 3.7, 5.2-5.3
FACT: the `resuming` watchdog write carries no generation bump; only the terminal snapshot-close and adoption bump it — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:241, spec/07_session-lifecycle.md:221
WATCHOUT: SPEC-3o cites **Resume driver.** items 1 and 2 by number; do not renumber items 1-4.

### [spec.5.review-kubernetes.1]
FACT: The only CRD surface 0092's staging touches is the pod release in SPEC-5b step 2 ("recycling pool recycles, any other pod drains"). That maps onto existing gateway-owned SandboxClaim writes: on a recycling pool the claim is patched bound -> recycling, and otherwise the claim is deleted or set to a terminal disposition while the pod projects claimed. The WarmPoolController projects the second case to draining. No new CRD field, status writer, or RBAC verb is needed. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:409-418 (occupancy projection), :616-621 (field ownership), :632 (gateway SA grants)
FACT: Suspension reason, lease, retry accounting, and the resume_pending handoff all live in Postgres and Redis (session row, REG-COORDLEASE). None of them is a CRD status, so the status-as-command-channel and finalizer idioms do not apply. POST /resume answers 200 at resume_pending, and the driver restores asynchronously, so no controller reconcile sits on the request path. — EVIDENCE: proposal spec-changes.md:63, :137
DECISION: returned no Kubernetes-idiom findings in round 5 — BECAUSE the r3->r5 diff only adds the §29.5 fence-citation replacement (SPEC-2c item 1), which is outside this lens — ALTERNATIVES: filing driver-release-of-claimed-pod drain waste (rejected: matches the existing claim-deleted-while-claimed projection and is not a defect)

### [spec.5.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: Returned no findings for the staged spec edits under the reliability lens — BECAUSE every recovery path traced (driver crash before and after the claim, watchdog-vs-attempt race on one replica, podless adoption without a fence, suspension release crash, budget-key re-arm) either serializes on the session row or falls to an existing reclaimer (§4.6.1 claim GC predicate 1, §6.2 `resuming` watchdog) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the narrow windows below as spec findings (their remedies are code, or they repeat refuted materiality cases).
FACT: A replacement pod is not named in `pod_assignment` during `resuming` in the current tree either: `bumpRecoveryGeneration` (the PodAssignment write) runs after `binder.Resume` returns. So CODE-4 moving it to the commit adds no new claim-GC exposure. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/resume_rebind.go:129; spec/04_system-components.md:517.
FACT: Same-replica attempt overlap is safe at row level. A watchdog `resuming → resume_pending` does not bump `coordination_generation`, but the `running` commit guard on `State == resuming` serializes a stale attempt against a fresh one, and the loser releases its pod. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1b preamble; non-spec-changes.md CODE-4 item 5.2.
FACT: `resume_pending` rows keep the failed pod in `PodAssignment` by design (the failure reporters stamp it), so "no bound pod" in SPEC-2b has to be read as "binding released" per §28.5.1 CH-ATTACH, not as an empty column. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/sessionserver/failure.go:337-338; spec/28_communication-channels.md:261.
UNVERIFIED: The CODE-6 `SuspendIdle` turn check and guarded write do not take the per-session delivery lock. A message classified `running` just before the write can start a turn on a pod that the next tick checkpoints and releases. The tier-7a test claims it never loses the message, but no mechanism is staged. The non-spec loop should check whether message delivery stamps `LastAgentActivityAt` before it sends. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 5; pkg/gateway/session/sessionidle/sessionidle.go:234.
UNVERIFIED: The podless message branch writes `resume_pending` and then enqueues to the DLQ (CODE-6 item 7). A driver that restores and drains before the enqueue strands the message in the DLQ of a `running` session. The window is tiny, so the non-spec loop should decide whether to enqueue first.
UNVERIFIED: The CODE-4 lease gate treats a lease read error as "not holder". During a Redis outage this stops every resume driver, while spec/12_storage-architecture.md:217 says leases fall back to Postgres. The non-spec loop owns this.

DECISION: returned no findings under the security lens — BECAUSE every staged bound stays gateway-sourced and fails closed: retryCount and the suspended-lifetime anchor live on the Postgres session row, a missed budget-key re-arm ends in BUDGET_KEYS_EXPIRED (cascade cancel plus the DelegationBudgetKeysExpired alert), the held-pod off-holder /resume fails closed, the SPEC-5b release drains non-recycling pods, and the resume claim keeps the §5.2 tenant-pin read (SPEC-3h, where a failed read claims nothing) — ALTERNATIVES: filing the playground session now persisting in suspended for 7 days after browser close (§27.6) was rejected as "less strict" under the settled owner decision that idle leads to suspension
FACT: an `input_required` session stays bounded after SPEC-5e marks the idle clock "Not evaluated", because the maxSessionAge table counts `input_required` time as Running — EVIDENCE: spec/06_warm-pod-model.md:255
FACT: the SPEC-2b podless fence skip opens no new window, because §10.1.2 step 3 already lets a pod accept RPCs of any generation for a session it holds no fenced generation for, and every fresh resume pod is in that state today — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md §10.1.2 step 3 ("A session bound to the pod for which the pod holds no fenced generation")
UNVERIFIED: in the §27.6 "Server-side session-record lifecycle" bullet, "idle timeout" probably means the playground cookie-record idle rather than maxClientIdleSeconds; if it means the latter, idle suspension now triggers bearer revocation; whoever owns the playground lens should check

### [redesign.1]
FACT: Redesign 1 (scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md) was applied on 2026-10-08. Every anchor matched once, and no edit was skipped. Its sentence after edit S5 ("The session's DLQ holds the messages buffered ...") is commentary for the redesign's reader and was not inserted.
DECISION: Resume-attempt supersession was redesigned — BECAUSE the 2026-10-07 run stopped on an ABA-prone SPEC-1b write condition. The `resume_pending → resuming` write now increments `coordination_generation` (SPEC-1b, SPEC-1f for §4.2 and §10.1.1, CODE-4 item 3.4), every exit from `resuming` is conditioned on that incarnation (SPEC-1b **Exits from `resuming`.**, CODE-4 items 3.7 and 5.2), the resumed binding is stamped with the post-entry generation (CODE-4 item 5.4), and the attempt that claimed the replacement pod is its only releaser (CODE-4 item 5.3).
DECISION: The `resuming` watchdog was redesigned — BECAUSE `sweepResuming` wrote the row on a state miss, took the retry branch from the listed row, and counted a report-produced `resume_pending` row as a watchdog retry. Its exit is now guarded on the listed row's generation, computes the retry branch from the locked row, and returns `errResumingSuperseded` on a miss (CODE-4 item 15).
DECISION: The parked residue of a `suspended` session was redesigned — BECAUSE a held-pod resume fault and an off-holder fallback buffered into a replica-local inbox no coordinator reads. Every undelivered message to a `suspended` session now waits in the session's DLQ (SPEC-5i, SPEC-5l, CODE-6 items 6, 7, and 12), `deliverBuffered` sweeps expired entries before it drains (CODE-4 item 6), and SPEC-5z widens the `dlq_ttl_expired` definitions and the terminal DLQ drain to a `suspended` target.
DECISION: The redesign also made SPEC-5m a pointer at the §6.2 idle rule, staged the SPEC-5x carriers in CODE-6 item 13 and DOCS-1b, stopped `ReattachNode` from calling `resumeOnPod` (CODE-4 item 11), and filed RECORDS-1 items 7(f) and 7(g).
FACT: Deleted by the redesign: the SPEC-1b "at most one attempt per session at a time" and "ends no later than the `resuming` watchdog's deadline" claims and its state-plus-unchanged-generation condition; the CODE-4 item 3.4 claim that the post-hook deadline matches the `sweepResuming` anchor; the optional `ExpectedCoordinationGeneration` semantics; the `applyFailureFromResuming` generation bump; the `sweepResuming` no-op write and its `budget <= 0` return; the pre-entry binding stamps in `resume_rebind.go`; the CODE-5 item 2 post-handoff binding generation; the §4.2 and §10.1.1 "first handoff mints 2" statements; the `ReattachNode` `resumeOnPod` call; the inbox fallback for a `suspended` target; the MCP executor path for a `suspended` target; SPEC-5m's restatement of the idle rule; and the `"idle"` trigger value in its carriers.
FACT: Reconciled after the edits: summary decisions 11, 13, and 15, two watch-outs, two defects entries for RECORDS-1 items 7(f) and 7(g), and the SPEC-1, SPEC-5, and CODE-4 index lines; checklist steps S5, S6, S11, and S13, and tier 3 on S12 for the `deadline_approaching` schema narrowing; the spec-changes Design paragraphs for SPEC-1 and SPEC-5; the non-spec Design watchdog bullet; the CODE-6 podless-message test order; a TEST-1 tier-3 check of the `deadline_approaching` enum; and the DOCS-1b content paragraph.
OPEN: OD-1, OD-2, and OD-3 are recorded under Standing context `### Open`, each with its default, which the staged files implement.

### [redesign.2]
FACT: Redesign 1 of the 2026-10-08 redesign run (scratchpad/redesign/0092_new_write-the-session-suspend-and-resume-proposal-that-gateway-redesign-1.md) was applied on 2026-10-08. Every anchor matched once, and no edit was skipped. Its commentary sentences after edit 7a, after edit 19 ("SPEC-5q edits other rows of §16.1. The two edits do not overlap."), and in edit 16 (withdrawn) were not inserted.
DECISION: Message custody across suspend and resume was redesigned — BECAUSE the 2026-10-08 run stopped on a held message that could be both forwarded and enqueued, a drain that wrote back to a per-replica inbox, and an enqueue that could land after the drain. SPEC-5i delivery path 6 is now the one home of custody: synchronous delivery only for a REST message the coordinating replica serves while the row names a pod, every other message held in the DLQ before any state write, a backlog resume by the coordinating replica's suspended sweep (CODE-6 item 2), the `holdMessage` re-read and all-or-nothing removal (CODE-4 item 7), a `deliverBuffered` that pops one DLQ entry at a time and restores the unattempted ones (CODE-4 item 6), an unconditional per-session delivery lock on both message paths, and `resuming` added to the recovering states (SPEC-5z items 2 to 6, CODE-6 item 7).
DECISION: Idle suspension was made atomic with a turn — BECAUSE no delivery stamps `LastAgentActivityAt`, `Stamp` is asynchronous, and the turn token is free between turns of one batch. `PodExecutor.Send` makes the synchronous `StampSync` admission write and refuses a `suspended` row, `TurnInFlight` covers the whole `Send`, and a refused batch is routed again (SPEC-5f, CODE-6 items 5 and 7).
DECISION: The readers of `coordination_generation` were reconciled with the entry increment — BECAUSE `peerTookOver` read a per-replica record that goes stale across a same-replica rebind and `RecordHandoff` incremented unconditionally. CODE-5 items 2a and 2b compare against the binding stamp and make `RecordHandoff` a compare-and-swap; SPEC-1f items 5 and 6 reword the §7.1 and §16.1 derive-failure fence; SPEC-1f item 3 adds the §10.1.5 **Session with no bound pod.** paragraph; CODE-4 item 17 rewrites the `derive.go` comments; and the barrier generation lag is RECORDS-1 finding 7(h).
DECISION: An off-holder `POST /resume` of a held-pod `suspended` session fails closed with `TARGET_NOT_READY` (CODE-4 item 8), because a drain off the lease holder fails every `Send` in `streamFor`. RECORDS-1 finding 7(b) records the missing forward.
FACT: Deleted by the redesign: the off-holder forward of a `suspended` message and its unreachable-coordinator fallback, the **Pod still held:** and **Coordinator routing for a suspended-session resume:** passages, and the split §29.3 `suspended` message rows; `DrainForResume` and its test; `clearInboxOnResume` with its call and tests; the `bufferTargetInbox` fallback and the inbox branch of `bufferAfterResumedSendFailure` for a `suspended` row; `SuspendedResumer`, replaced by `SessionDelivery`; the lease-conditioned "message path lock"; the `renewedGeneration` record and the unconditional `RecordHandoff`; the edge case "`RecordHandoff` during an incarnation"; and the enqueue-after-drain finding 7(g), rewritten as the held-message residual. RECORDS-1 gains findings 7(h) (barrier generation lag) and 7(i) (no `inbox_cleared` at lease takeover).
FACT: Reconciled after the edits: summary What-changes bullet for CODE-6, decision 4 (off-holder held-pod `/resume` fails closed), new decisions 16 (admission write) and 17 (binding stamp and `RecordHandoff` compare-and-swap), the 0058 and 0091 impact rows, and the CODE-4 and CODE-6 index lines; checklist steps S5 (SPEC-5i2), S11, and S12; the spec-changes Design paragraphs for SPEC-1 and SPEC-5 and the SPEC-5z heading; the non-spec CODE-4 item 9 callers, the CODE-6 item 7 `SessionDelivery` sentence, the CODE-6 item 12 rationale, the held-pod `/resume` tier-1 and tier-3 expectations, `TestPath6ResumeNotPerformedEnqueuesDLQ`, and `sessiondeps.go` in the files touched.
OPEN: OD-A (the per-`Send` admission write cost) and OD-B (the `errResumeHeldPodReleased → enterResumePending` branch) are recorded under Standing context `### Open`, each with its default, which the staged files implement.
WATCHOUT: The four `Replaced:` lines added to Standing context `### Traps` name the replaced forward, drain, generation record, and idle-atomicity mechanisms and why each lost. Do not restore any of them.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-5f **Idle suspension.** routes a refused delivery "by §7.2 delivery path 6" — BECAUSE path 6 is the one home of custody; the lead covers a non-drain refusal and the drain bullet covers a drained DLQ entry — ALTERNATIVES: re-sync SPEC-5f with the drain outcome (two copies of one rule).
DECISION: **Enqueue after a drain.** retracts on a terminal re-read too, so the found message is routed by path 7 (synchronous TARGET_TERMINAL) — ALTERNATIVES: emit `message_expired`/`target_terminated` from the re-read (rejected: a third emitter that spec/07 §7.2 reason list and spec/15 §15.4 omit, and an async answer to a sender `holdMessage` can still answer synchronously).

DECISION: The inbox stage of the drain removes each entry only after delivery, per the §7.2 durable-inbox Explicit ACK row, in both inbox modes, through new `Inbox.Peek`/`Inbox.Ack`; at-most-once and restore apply to DLQ entries only — BECAUSE `RedisInbox.Drain` is LRANGE+DEL (pkg/gateway/session/sessioninbox/sessioninbox.go Drain), which contradicts at-least-once ACK and the durable no-drop guarantee (spec/07 §7.2 "does not drop undelivered messages as long as Redis remains available") — ALTERNATIVES: migrate inbox to DLQ first (still at-most-once), durable-only branch (mode branch).
WATCHOUT: The drain's refusal sentence ("It also returns the message whose delivery failed when the gateway refused...") now effectively applies to DLQ entries only; an inbox entry is never removed before delivery, so there is nothing to return. Do not add an inbox restore. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-5i **Delivery after a commit to `running`.**
